# Multi-Level Work Breakdown Structure (WBS)

> **Companion design document**. This document is a companion to
> [RFC-039 Compiler Architecture Restructuring](../../rfc/accepted/039-compiler-architecture.md). It
> expands the RFC’s phase sequence into **three levels of tasks that can be committed and verified
> independently**.
>
> RFC-039’s main text is the sole authority for phase order and acceptance gates. This document is
> responsible only for decomposition to an executable granularity and for registering conflicts that
> have not yet been adjudicated.

## Positioning and Scope

| Level       | Count       | Meaning                                                                                          |
| ----------- | ----------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| **Level 1** | 11 (P0–P10) | Phases, in one-to-one correspondence with RFC-039’s phase sequence                               |
| **Level 2** | 73          | Task groups: work groups decomposed by phase, each able to be staffed and archived independently |
| **Level 3** | 261         | Work units that can be committed and verified independently                                      |

**Reserved gaps (not reused)**: P4 has no `4.7`/`4.8`, and P8 has no `8.1`; after the LALRPOP route
was withdrawn, `8.4` / `8.5` / `8.6` were also left unused (2026-11-25). All three have had no
corresponding entries since the WBS was established—the entire history of this document has zero
matches for the three headings `**4.7` / `**4.8` / `**8.1`
(`git log -S'**4.7' -- docs/src/dev/architecture/09-execution-wbs.md` can verify this), and there
are no references anywhere else in the repository. `8.1` corresponds exactly to stage 1 of `05`
(test revival and completion): that stage was split into P1 (`1.1`–`1.3`) and `8.8`, so P8’s
numbering aligns with its phase numbers (phase 0 → `8.0`, phase 2 → `8.2`, and so on).

**Covered by this document**: task decomposition, dependencies, parallelization groups, and binding
to acceptance criteria.

**Not covered by this document**: the design rationale for tasks (see `02`–`08`) or the
justification for phase ordering (see RFC-039).

**Registration convention (effective 2026-10-12)**: every newly registered level-2/level-3 row
**must contain two fields**—**“Owning phase”** (the P phase to which it truly belongs, which may
differ from the current section) and **“Blocks the mainline”**. `Registered, awaiting scheduling` is
no longer the default state; an unmarked entry is considered **not schedulable**. IDs must be unique
across the entire document (cross-track tasks use a track ID such as `029g.x` and must not occupy an
`N.x` ID).

**Closure registration (effective 2026-11-24)**: a checked row contains only four items—the task ID,
status, **implementation commit short hash** (7 characters), and issue number (if any)—on one line:
`- [x] **3.24.1 Remaining pattern-matching work** ✅ 4d608df3 (issue #405)`. **Do not write the fix,
the criterion chain, gate values, file line numbers, test names, or review conclusions**: those
belong in the commit message (`git show <short-hash>`). This table is a board, not a process
chronicle. **Do not write ruling labels or restate conclusions**: “User ruling A/B/C,” “Plan A,”
“Option A,” “The final implementation is…” and similar wording do not belong here—the board answers
only “Is it complete?” Rulings and results are recorded in commit messages and the RFC-039 decision
registry.

**Short hashes are determined after pushing**: a closure row records the short hash on `origin/dev`
after the push completes. The order is **push the implementation commit first, then write the
closure row** (see “Commit hash is obtained after push” in
[commit-convention](../commit-convention.md)). The closure commit itself carries no hash—a
self-reference is impossible.

**Count columns contain numbers only**: the level-1/level-2/level-3 counts in “Positioning and
Scope” contain only current values; change logs such as `+1 = …` do not belong in the table.

## Current State: Gaps Found During Decomposition

While consolidating the “Implementation Points” from eight documents into this table, **five hard
gaps in the documentation system itself** were found. All have now been resolved:

| #   | Gap                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Resolution                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| --- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| G1  | **P5 has no construction steps at all.** The RFC defines P5 (splitting the checker) and its acceptance criterion (C1 zero-diff), but none of the seven documents contains a section describing how to perform the split; `01-routing.md` contains a single line for `annotations.rs` in its directory tree, the only mention in the entire batch                                                                                          | The P5 steps are supplied by this document (see below)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| G2  | **The `include!` refactor has three claimed owners and no implementation.** The RFC places it in P4; `02` assigns the reference to itself; `06` also lists it under S7. However, `02`’s change list (6 additions + 20 modifications) **does not contain `semantic_tokens.rs`**                                                                                                                                                            | The construction steps are assigned to §P5 5.1 in this document (after P4 is complete); references in the RFC and `06` have been redirected here                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| G3  | **Three overlapping authoritative lists of CI scripts.** The `RFC` lists 4 `scripts/ci/*.py` scripts; `01` lists 4 scripts + 1 `cargo test`; `07` lists 4 checks under entirely different names; `04` previously listed `check-synth-boundary.py` (that script was cancelled by the user on 2026-11-24). After deduplication, there are **at least 9 new scripts and no unified list**; `check-obligations.py` is owned by both P4 and P9 | See the unified list in §P9 of this document (10 active scripts, each owned by one phase); all other documents now reference this table                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| G4  | **Conflict over the life or death of `precedence_inline.rs`.** `06` counts it among the “1,005 lines to be revived,” while B6 in the same document says it “must be deleted together with” the `Precedence` enum; `05` advocates deletion                                                                                                                                                                                                 | **Revive in P1, retain in P8** (decision registry C1; Pratt is no longer to be deleted)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| G5  | **Conflict in the wording of the L2 goal.** The RFC and this directory’s index say “grammar-driven syntax,” but `05` self-assesses as **“not actually using true grammar-driven parsing (LALRPOP); it only achieved declarative table lookup”**                                                                                                                                                                                           | Resolved as **a declarative precedence table + an unambiguous language surface**: full LALRPOP grammar-driven parsing was withdrawn (the two attempts produced 29 / 148 conflicts), and the implementation fell back, as specified at 05:648, to “retain Pratt + one declarative precedence table + same-source cross-checking of two `BinOp` implementations”; see P8 8.3 |

## Target Design: Phase Sequence

```
P0 仓库维护机制与代码放置规程 08 ← 新增，先立规矩
P1 复活孤儿测试并修缺陷 06 §S1
P2 建立等价性判据基线 07 全部
P3 修正确性漏洞（最小方案） 02 §S3-漏洞半部
P4 阶段契约与统一 Driver 02
P5 checker 文件内拆分 02 + 本文补齐
P6 类型表示单一化 03
P7 中间表示 SSA 化 04 } 可并行
P8 前端范式变更 05 }
P9 防反弹门禁（含统一脚本清单） 01 §防反弹 + 08 §可机检规则
P10 其余清理与状态修正 06 §S2/S3/S4/S5/S6
```

**Rationale for adding P0**: the three prohibitions—no fabrication, no endless filling, and no
patching when a refactor is required—constrain every action in P1–P10. The rules must be established
before work begins; otherwise, work would already be adding `mod` declarations to `tests/mod.rs`
while reviving tests in P1—which is precisely what caused conflict G4.

### P3 Fix Correctness Vulnerabilities (Minimal Solution)

Source: [02](02-stage-contract.md) §Implementation Points S3, vulnerability-fix half ＋ **2026-10-07
silent-discard audit (3.4)**

> **Semantic-fix routing (D59, ruled on 2026-10-11)**: if this track or a subsequent phase discovers
> a defect for which “the old behavior does not count as success”—**A silent failure / B silent
> mismatch / C undefined behavior and uncontrolled resources** (crashes, stack exhaustion, or
> silently producing the wrong answer)—it **may be fixed alongside the refactor**, provided all four
> conditions are met: ① red before green (the red criterion must exist first and must actually be
> red); ② no change to the externally observable behavior of programs that “previously compiled
> successfully and ran correctly” (they must not become failures, and their existing diagnostic
> codes/prose/exit codes must not be removed or changed); ③ registration in the same commit (the row
> states the error category + a revision note for that phase + the PR’s required “Error Type” line);
> ④ baseline regeneration and manual diff review in the same commit. **D user-visible semantics / E
> external-contract tables / F RFC-level design changes must still be adjudicated first.** See
> [07 §Semantic-fix routing criteria](07-equivalence-oracle.md) for the expanded criteria and the
> A–F whitelist; **“default to a super-refactor and register it afterward” is not accepted** (the
> rationale is in RFC-039 D59: without a zero-red criterion, fixes cannot be distinguished from
> regressions, and this conflicts with both “no C5′” and “no relaxation of any kind”).

|                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | 3.20.20 Eq/Neq compile-time acceptance of cross-type and static-Void comparisons—after investigation, the **ruling package is presented and awaiting a ruling** (investigation and presentation are complete, issue [#549](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/549)). **The investigation falsified the premise that “this can be fixed along the way as category C”**: the probe matrix confirms that heterogeneously typed container Eq forms (`[1,2]==(1,2)` / `[1]==["x"]` / `[1]==[2.5]` / `(1,2)==(1,"x")`) compile successfully and **run successfully, printing false**—compile-time rejection would change the externally observable behavior of programs that previously ran successfully ⇒ D59 **category D**, which does not satisfy the conditions for an incidental fix; scalar cross-type / static-Void comparison / heterogeneously typed Dict comparison fall to E6007 at runtime (old behavior is not successful, so they may be fixed incidentally but do not constitute an independent ruling point); it was also observed that **same-typed** Dict==Dict also falls to E6007 at runtime (there is no comparison arm for Handle), so this is recorded but not adjudicated. The sole ruling point = whether cross-type container Eq should be rejected at compile time (Option A: compile-time rejection, with a same-type static discipline on both sides of full-range sorted comparison (the criterion body = infer_binary comparison arm)—recommended; Option B: retain runtime false and go through the design process); the complete four-part ruling package is in the comments on issue [#549](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/549). | 3.22 Decomposition semantics for function-type annotations (issue [#501](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/501); source = follow-up F4 from the round-2 batch review of 4.10): the **parenthesized function type** in the return position is decomposed at the wrong level—there is **no valid annotation syntax** for “a function that returns a function type”; the annotation itself is parsed correctly (the parser has been ruled out by measurement). **Owning phase: P3; blocks the mainline: no**                                        | 3.22.1 See below                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | `src/frontend/core/typecheck/inference/statements.rs`                                                                                                                                                                | —                                                                            | **Terminal criterion**: see 3.22.1 |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | 3.22.1 Unify annotation decomposition for “function type return”: the value-parameter group of `() -> ((x: Int) -> Int)` = `()`, and the return = `fn(int64)->int64`; the inner `Fn` **must not be absorbed into the parameter group**; align the expected convention on the lambda-body path accordingly (there are currently two conventions). **Acceptance**: vulnerability criteria turn green, and **intentionally removing the fix must turn
them red again**. **Risk**: the new E4018 on the multi-file path is a breaking change.

**3.4 acceptance**: 3.4.1 / 3.4.2 must have red criteria first (code changes before the criterion
are forbidden); 3.4.3 is a manual review; 3.4.4–3.4.8 are governed by C2/C3 and the 9.2 gate in P9.
**Risk**: the new diagnostic in 3.4.1 is a behavior change, so the corpus differential baseline and
CHANGELOG must be updated in sync.

> **Hemorrhage-control path**: P3 need not wait for all of P2. The only hard prerequisites for
> 3.1/3.2 are **2.3.2 (multi-file corpus layer) + 2.4.1/2.4.3 (two red criteria)**;
> 2.1/2.2/2.3.1/2.3.3 (IR verifier, snapshots, single-file differential, performance baseline) can
> proceed in parallel with P3. Stopping the bleeding from correctness vulnerabilities such as
> `Sorted(3)` silently passing must not be blocked by snapshot infrastructure. 3.3
> (panic-to-diagnostic) has no prerequisites and can be implemented at any time.

### External Track RFC-029g: Remove `pub` and Automatic Binding (External Track / #399 — Ordering Constraint D56)

Source: [RFC-029g](../../rfc/accepted/029g-remove-pub-and-auto-bind.md) (accepted 2026-10-02;
implementation not started), [#399](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/399). **This track
is not a design item of the refactor itself; it is a specification-compliance deletion forced by an
ordering constraint.** It is distinct from P10 S5’s “reduce the visibility of `pub` items”
(narrowing Rust’s `pub(crate)`).

**Why it must be completed in full before P4**: 029g’s deletion surface overlaps the rewrite
surfaces of all four refactoring phases. Reversing the order would mean repeating the same deletions
and baseline updates on the new grammar / new checker layout / new SSA IR.

| 029g deletion surface                                              | Files                                                                                                              | Rewritten by which phase                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `KwPub` and syntax branches                                        | `lexer/{tokens,state,mod}.rs`; `parser/parser_state.rs`, `statements/{declarations,imports,functions,bindings}.rs` | **P8** (8.2 lexical convergence / 8.3 Pratt precedence convergence / 8.7 split `parse_assign_after_target`)                                                                                                                                                                         |
| AST `is_pub` ×2 (`Assign` / `TypeDefinition`)                      | `parser/ast.rs`                                                                                                    | **P6 6.5 AST dead variants** + P8 + D1 directory renaming (`parser/ast.rs` → top-level `src/ast/`)                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `auto_bind_to_type` and the pub branch of `collect_exports`        | `typecheck/checker.rs`, `environment.rs`                                                                           | **P5** (the `include!`→real `mod` conversion and checker split are **pure moves**, and the pub branch is moved unchanged into the new file)                                                                                                                                         |
| Dead-code exemption `exempt_pub` / `is_exported` and role dispatch | `typecheck/passes/dead_code.rs`, `module/orchestrator.rs` (inside `check_project`)                                 | **P4 4.2.2** (slim down `check_project`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `Public` modifier in `semantic_tokens`                             | `typecheck/checker/semantic_tokens.rs`                                                                             | **P4 4.2.6** + P5 + 4.6 (D55)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `is_pub` binding ignored by `ir_gen`                               | `middle/core/ir_gen.rs`                                                                                            | **P7** (SSA conversion of `ir_gen.rs`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| 31 instances of `pub` in `src/std/`                                | `std/list.yx` / `json.yx` / `option.yx` / `result.yx`                                                              | No phase changes these directly, but **P6’s hard acceptance criterion requires zero differences in `src/std/`** (`git diff --stat src/std/` is empty; direct-drive tests under `tests/` are exempt as described in P6’s hard-acceptance section)—they can only be changed before P6 |

**Incidental benefit**: 029g deletes the dead `env.exports` table (populated on the production side
and consumed nowhere in the repository) and the `is_exported`/`is_visible` accessors ⇒ P4’s 4.3
obligation ledger loses one more “produced but never consumed” field.

- [x] **029g.1 Lexer and parser branches**
- [x] **029g.2 AST `is_pub` ×2**
- [x] **029g.3 Checker side**
- [x] **029g.4 Dead-code exemption convergence**
- [x] **029g.5 Peripheral output**
- [x] **029g.6 std and diagnostic prose**
- [x] **029g.7 Language-reference convergence**

| Level 2                    | Level 3                                                                                                                    | Files                                                                                                                | Acceptance                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| -------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 029g.1 Lexer and parser    | 029g.1.1 Delete the `KwPub` variant and its `keyword_from_str` entry                                                       | `lexer/tokens.rs`, `state.rs`, `mod.rs`                                                                              | Keyword table 18→17 (`Kw*` 16→15, including the count comment in `tokens.rs`)                                                                                                                                   |
|                            | 029g.1.2 Delete pub detection and `pre_detected_pub` at declaration positions                                              | `parser/statements/declarations.rs`, `functions.rs`, `bindings.rs`                                                   | `pub f = ...` reports a parse error (**red criterion first**)                                                                                                                                                   |
|                            | 029g.1.3 Delete pub skipping for entries inside `use` braces and leading pub before statements                             | `parser/statements/imports.rs`, `parser_state.rs`                                                                    | `pub use m` reports a parse error (**red criterion first**)                                                                                                                                                     |
| 029g.2 AST                 | 029g.2.1 Delete `Assign`/`TypeDefinition` `is_pub` + synchronize construction sites                                        | `parser/ast.rs` and all construction sites                                                                           | No pub remnants in the compiler (`grep -rn is_pub src/` is empty)                                                                                                                                               |
| 029g.3 Checker             | 029g.3.1 Delete `auto_bind_to_type` (pub was its sole reason for existing)                                                 | `checker.rs`, `environment.rs`, call site `checker.rs`                                                               | Method forms support only explicit composition (RFC-004); zero dependencies in std or the corpus                                                                                                                |
|                            | 029g.3.2 Remove the pub branch from `collect_exports` + delete the dead `env.exports` table and `is_exported`/`is_visible` | `checker.rs`, `environment.rs`                                                                                       | The sole export authority = the module registry `ModuleInfo.exports`; the 9.2 gate has one fewer field                                                                                                          |
| 029g.4 Dead-code exemption | 029g.4.1 Delete `exempt_pub` / `is_exported` and the exemption branch                                                      | `dead_code.rs`                                                                                                       | Unreferenced top-level bindings in Lib/Script roles report W1001 (**red criterion first**)                                                                                                                      |
|                            | 029g.4.2 Synchronize `orchestrator` role dispatch                                                                          | `orchestrator.rs`                                                                                                    | Single-file / multi-file diagnostic sets are normalized (C2); W codes do not mix into consumer-side diagnostics                                                                                                 |
| 029g.5 Peripheral output   | 029g.5.1 The formatter’s pub prefix                                                                                        | `formatter/handlers/stmt.rs`                                                                                         | Formatting is idempotent (existing criterion)                                                                                                                                                                   |
|                            | 029g.5.2 The `Public` modifier in `semantic_tokens`                                                                        | `typecheck/checker/semantic_tokens.rs`                                                                               | Semantic highlighting no longer produces Public                                                                                                                                                                 |
| 029g.6 std and prose       | 029g.6.1 Delete 31 pub prefixes in `src/std`                                                                               | `std/{list,json,option,result}.yx`                                                                                   | The corpus and std self-checks are all green; **must be completed before P6**                                                                                                                                   |
|                            | 029g.6.2 Diagnostic prose                                                                                                  | 4 W1xxx help messages                                                                                                | They no longer claim that “pub is an external interface”; aligned with the Bin-role behavior in `dead_code.rs`                                                                                                  |
| 029g.7 Documentation       | 029g.7.1 Language reference and guides                                                                                     | `reference/language-spec/{modules,syntax,language-overview}.md`, `warning-code/warning-codes.md`, `guide/modules.md` | The specification no longer contains statements contradicting the implementation by saying that “pub can still be written”; the “pub never triggers W1001” statement in `warning-codes.md` is corrected as well |

**Acceptance (behavior changes; red criterion first)**:

1. **Red before green**: land a red criterion for each of the two behavior changes before changing
   code—① `pub` changes from “silently accepted” to a parse error; ② unreferenced top-level bindings
   in Lib/Script roles newly report W1001 (previously pub items were absolutely exempt).
2. **The test count must not decrease**: when deleting the `exempt_pub` case in
   `typecheck/passes/tests/dead_code.rs` and the `is_pub` fixture, equivalent new cases must replace
   them.
3. **Baseline in the same commit**: the diagnostic-set changes (new parse error + new W1001) ⇒
   update the corpus differential baseline (`tests/baselines/corpus-parity.jsonl`) and CHANGELOG in
   the same commit; the IR snapshot is expected to have zero diff, and any non-zero diff must be
   explained.
4. **Independent revert unit**: the entire batch occupies one commit group and must not be mixed
   with other phases (DoD 6).
5. `cargo test` / `clippy -D warnings` / `python scripts/rfc/check_tracking.py` are all green.

**Ordering**: it overlaps files with 3.4 (`checker.rs`, `environment.rs`) ⇒ the two must be serial
and cannot run in parallel; **the entire track precedes P4** (RFC-039 D56). The 029g track does not
depend on 3.4; implementers may choose which to begin based on the context at hand.

> **Commit correspondence (2026-10-08)**: the incorporation of this section (the P3.5 chapter and
> main-chain/constraint row) was included in `97071c81b` together with RFC-039’s D56 when the
> parallel-stream cleanup workspace was merged; that commit’s message records “3.4 supplementary
> registration + D57/D58 rulings recorded,” and does not mention this track. History is not
> rewritten. It is registered in place following the established convention (the `d5b18390`
> precedent, see the P2 implementation note), serving as evidence for later bisecting.

|                                                   | 4.4.4 The shared-solver observation ledger `ConsumerStats`/`CONSUMER_STATS`: every production point writes, while the production side has zero readers (the only reader is `proof/smt/tests/shared.rs`). **Ruling of 2026-10-24 ①: retain it**—write “the sole consumer = tests” into the doc contract (preserving the D58 observable-criterion carrier); ruling ② “delete it” is not adopted (the acceptance criterion of 4.4.2 would also have to be rewritten, and the trade-off rationale is recorded in the 4.4 closure “Item 16”). **Terminal state**: three contract sentences are written into the module header of `src/frontend/core/typecheck/proof/smt/backend.rs` and the relevant item documentation—① the write surface = one atomic increment per acquisition through the shared outlet; ② the read surface is **only** `proof/smt/tests/shared.rs` (zero calls from production code); ③ `SolverConsumer::ALL` is a **test-only constant**, and production code must not depend on it; **zero behavior change** (only comments changed, and the acceptance numbers for 4.4.2 remain unchanged; issue [#475](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/475)) |                                                   | 4.9.9 A cross-unit single-definition method called with **named arguments** loses its formal-parameter-name table (registered 2026-11-25, discovered during the 7c closure): calling a cross-unit method (injected through the registry) with named arguments reports `E1014 Function '…' has no parameter named '…' (available: )`—the name table is empty; positional calls to the same method work normally, as do named-argument calls within a single file. Minimal reproduction: `lib.yx` defines `Point.scale_by: (self: &Point, k: Float) -> Float`, and `main.yx` contains `p.scale_by(k = 2.0)` ⇒ E1014, while `p.scale_by(2.0)` ⇒ 6.0. **D59 category: C** (old behavior is unsuccessful = compile-time false rejection; the mechanism is a **consumer-side key mismatch**—the IR-generation-side formal-name query has only three sources, “this unit’s `fn_param_names` / leaf name / module export surface”; the first segment of the method key `{Type}.{method}` is a type name, not a module key, so it never matches the export surface; this is not an externally observable change to previously successful programs). **Two root causes**: ① `AstToIrGenerator::lookup_param_info` (`src/middle/lower/query.rs`) lacks a consumer path from “defining-unit qualified name → formal-name table of the overload candidate group”; ② the narrowing rule ② in `visible_method_overloads` pairs by local name ⇒ candidates for an alias import (`use lib.{Point as P}`) are never injected. **Fix (single point; do not introduce a second name table)**: ① add `method_param_info` to `lookup_param_info`, obtaining the formal-name table from the existing sole cross-unit carrier `ModuleInfo::method_overloads` (candidate ↔ mangled name ↔ formal names as three aligned vectors; `extract_module_info` ← `method_overload_snapshot`, the same source as the typecheck injection surface) using the key `{module}.{Type}.{method}[#N]`; change the call site (`src/middle/lower/expr/call.rs`) to use the defining-unit qualified name from `method_dispatch_name` as the lookup key; ② change rule ② to pair by target (definition-side type name); ③ add **provider priority** to narrowing rule ③: a bare-name provider (`local == target`) takes priority, and an alias fills the position only when the bare name is absent (an alias does not create a bare name and must not participate in the “bare-name ambiguity across multiple modules” determination). **Fallback (“do not silently discard named arguments”)**: `reject_named_args_without_param_names` reuses the empty-table exit of the shared criterion `arg_binding::bind_args_to_params` (the existing E1014, with `available` using the same convention = empty string), and connect it to the four IR-side arms that have named arguments but cannot obtain a formal-name table—RFC-004 binding arm, namespace arm, ordinary method arm without a resolution, and ordinary **static** function arm; the built-in container-constructor arm (`src/middle/lower/expr/container.rs`) likewise rejects named arguments other than `len`. **Visible changes (list each separately; do not merge them into “no behavior change”)**: ① named arguments for cross-unit methods (bare import / alias import / two overload candidates) change from E1014 with an empty table → binding by name (measured 6.0 / 30); ② named arguments for a g9g generic curried method change from **silently discarded arguments** (`b.v` reads Void, producing runtime `E6007 Void vs Int`) → compile-time E1014 (category C; its formal-name table was left empty during registration because the curried signature did not align, which is another root cause); ③ named arguments for a cross-unit ordinary function change from silently discarded arguments → E1014; ④ named arguments for a const-generic receiver method change from silently discarded arguments → E1014 (the **positional** form of the same shape still fails, which is an existing independent defect and outside this task); ⑤ **mixed alias + bare name with the same target and positional-argument overload**: fix round 2 once degraded to E1002 (`t.bump(3.0)`, a regression relative to pre-t1) because rule ③ skipped the entire name; fix round 3 corrected it (bare-name provider takes priority) → restored to `0/0`; ⑥ named arguments in a built-in container constructor, `Vec(Int)(foo = 3)`, change from silently ignored → E1014 (category C; `Vec(Int)(len = 3)` remains 0/0); ⑦ named arguments with an entire-module import (`use lib` + `lib.Circle(...)`) change from silently discarded arguments → E1014 (the t1 report previously stated that “an entire-module import still silently discards named arguments”; this is now obsolete and is corrected in place); ⑧ named arguments in the RFC-004 external-binding form → E1014 (its **positional** arguments have a separate existing E6006 defect unrelated to named arguments). **Residual / known boundaries**: named arguments in calls inside closures and nested lambda function bodies **retain the old behavior (silently ignored)**—a zero-parameter closure `f = () => 7; g = f(x = 1)` compiled successfully before the fix and produced the correct result, so reclassifying it as a compile error is D59 category D and outside this task’s scope; it is registered as a known gap (D60 ② should report but does not, category A); `E1014` prose limitation: an empty `available` means “the IR did not obtain a name table,” not “the function truly has no such parameter”; this task **does not change the prose or expand the diagnostic surface** (known observation); when imports are mixed, the alias-side instance and the bare-name-side instance share the same bare-name candidate key (ambiguity at the name layer originates in the source program itself), and candidates use the bare-name provider. **Criteria**: driver-level direct-drive `src/driver/tests/named_args_cross_unit_method.rs` (cross-unit named arguments / alias import / overload candidate selection by name / mixed import + positional argument + two-candidate value assertions / entire-module import / RFC-004 binding / old closure behavior / container-constructor rejection + `len` positive control), unit guard `src/middle/lower/tests/query.rs` (empty name table ⇒ `None`; deleting the guard turns it red), and project fixtures `tests/yaoxiang-multifile/named-args-cross-unit-method{,-alias-overload}`. **Completion revision**: implementation `02ed42d2`, fix round 2 `091b835b`, fix round 3 (t3’s F1/F2/F3 and this section) = the commit to which this row belongs (the short hash will be supplied by the t4 closure). **Regression surface**: zero IR-snapshot drift; corpus parity has 405 entries (403 existing entries byte-for-byte unchanged + 2 new fixtures). | 4.9.7         | See the criterion in `src/driver/tests/named_args_cross_unit_method.rs` and the “Criteria” column of this row; see the left cell’s “Visible changes / Residual” section for the boundary list                                                                         |

### P5 Split the Checker into Files — Supplied by This Document (Original Gap G1)

Source: RFC definition + the `01-routing.md` directory tree + hard constraint (the `pub(crate)` path
of `collect_used_in_type` must not change)

- [x] **5.1 `include!` → real `mod`** (2 level-3 tasks)
- [x] **5.2 Extract `refinement`** (2 level-3 tasks)
- [x] **5.3 Extract `annotations`** (2 level-3 tasks)

| Level 2                     | Level 3                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Prerequisites | Acceptance                                                                                                                                        |
| --------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 5.1 `include!` → real `mod` | 5.1.1 Change the `include!` at the end of `checker.rs` to a real `mod` declaration (do not hard-code the line anchor; at the time of measurement, it was at the end of the file)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | P4 complete   | C1 zero-diff; `semantic_tokens.rs` acquires module identity                                                                                       |
|                             | 5.1.2 Add `use super::*` or individual imports (`include!` implicitly inherited the enclosing scope)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | 5.1.1         | `cargo build` is green                                                                                                                            |
| 5.2 Extract `refinement`    | 5.2.1 Move the refinement blocks out (three non-contiguous ranges in the original `checker.rs`; now the single file `checker/refinement.rs`, containing `ReturnRefinement` / `RefinedWalkCtx`), leaving the public surface and 6 cross-boundary shared items in place                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | 5.1.1         | C1 zero-diff                                                                                                                                      |
|                             | 5.2.2 Consolidate the refinement entry point into “`&self` + return diagnostics,” **without leaving the impl**: `collect_refined_binding_checks` (now `checker/refinement.rs`, `pub(super)`) no longer uses `&mut self` to write to the error channel implicitly; the diagnostic set is returned upward, and the single write point is in the sole caller `check_module_impl` (`checker.rs`, symbol location). **Rationale for remaining in the impl**: the only field used by this family is `self.env` (technically, leaving the impl would be feasible), but converting both the entry point and the walk to free functions would also move their call sites: 8 production sites for `resolve_type_annotation` (6 annotation paths + `refined_walk_stmt` + `collect_refined_var_names_in_stmt`), 2 production sites + 1 test for `collect_param_refinements`, and would create a reverse dependency from the “retention surface (signature collection) → refinement”—contrary to the coupling this step is intended to eliminate | 5.2.1         | —                                                                                                                                                 |
| 5.3 Extract `annotations`   | 5.3.1 Move out the 10-item annotation-validation family (now the single file `checker/annotations.rs`; `check_annotation_type_names` is the `pub(super)` entry point, the other 9 items are private to the family, and `is_predicate_head` is a free function within the family)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | 5.2.2         | C1 zero-diff                                                                                                                                      |
|                             | 5.3.2 **Keep `collect_used_in_type` (`pub(crate)`) as `pub(crate)` under the `checker` module path**: it is a **shared-surface** function (consumed by both `collect_function_signature` and `inference/statements.rs:16`), and 5.3.1 **did not migrate it** according to measurement ⇒ the “zero caller changes” criterion **was already satisfied**, so **do not add** a `pub(crate) use annotations::*;` re-export. **Constraint retained**: if it is moved with the family in the future (into annotations or another later batch), `checker.rs` must add a `pub(crate) use annotations::*;` re-export so that the import remains unchanged                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | 5.3.1         | **“Zero caller changes” is the hard acceptance criterion for this step: `git diff src/frontend/core/typecheck/inference/statements.rs` is empty** |

**Hard constraint**: `collect_used_in_type` is used across modules by `inference/statements.rs:16`.
**If** the function is moved with the annotation family in the future, **then** `checker.rs` must
add a `pub(crate) use annotations::*;` re-export so that the import remains unchanged. 5.3 did
**not** move it, so this batch adds no re-export (the criterion was already satisfied).

**Only split confirmed large blocks.** Do not split modules that are only 200–400 lines—this
increases navigation cost, reduces cohesion, and yields zero decoupling benefit. The remaining
submodules (signatures / type_defs / imports) are to be split after the files grow back to 1,500
lines.

**6.9 Model and Invariants (First-Principles Rewrite, 2026-11-13)**

> **Model**: D62’s end state is a **unified item world**—type positions and value positions share
> one set of compile-time items. One strength declaration produces one quantifier reading
> `q ∈ {0,1,ω}`; `q` is the **sole input** to slot allocation, and the call-site actual view and
> implementation-header binding view are two projections of the same fact. Equality is a **pure
> decision** (the two sides are identical after normalization; it does not bind type variables);
> evidence is the **sole entry point** for q=0 slots (four sources: literals / value environment /
> single-value pinning / Γ equalities); erasure is the **runtime consequence** of q=0 (both closed
> terms and open/recursive terms); Σ’s constructor sites **reuse** the same evidence interface and
> do not establish a separate mechanism. **Completion criterion = the invariants hold, not merely
> that subphase names have run.**

| Invariant                 | Content                                                                                                                                                                                 | Current State                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Phase |
| ------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----- |
| I1 Single source          | There is one write point/read point for q facts, the two views, and slot allocation; no second ledger                                                                                   | 6.9.2-A/P0/C2b established; **the representation of open terms in C3 has been decided and implemented** (`GenericArg::Const(ConstExpr)` is the sole representation, `normalize` is the sole evaluator, and mono’s binding source remains only `name_map`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | A     |
| I2 Pure equality          | defeq does not bind variables; different Refined predicates ⇒ Unproven; a decision point first unifies, and after failure rolls back all bindings before defeq; one budget per decision | 6.9.3a/b/c kernel established; **6.9.3d has been connected to production decision points** (`solver.unify`’s unify→rollback→defeq; the rollback and budget protocols were formalized in the 6.9.3d implementation-round §2)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | B     |
| I3 Complete evidence      | Evidence for q=0 slots comes only from four sources; no evidence ⇒ E1113 at the call site, never silent                                                                                 | Function/method surfaces established (6.9.2-B, #538); **the Γ source is established (6.9.4, 2026-11-15) and the F-1 shadowing-leak defect fixed (2026-11-16)**; the import surface #539 remains open                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | C     |

| I6 Σ                      | A field may refer to prior fields; constructor sites verify each field; witness erasure ≠ runtime check                                                                                 | B.0 design is complete; B.1a/b/c, B.2a/B.2b, B.3, and B.4 are **all implemented**; the residual supplementary-test round is closed (F10 fix + T06/T15/T16/T18/T23 direct-drive tests; F11/F12 registered only); **the imported-source prerequisite has been fixed by binding identity (same-name, different bindings must not be mismatched)**                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | D     |
| I7 Real wiring            | Decisions connect to real production decision points (`solver.unify`), with production call-graph evidence                                                                              | **d is complete**: 19 decisions / 15 probes / 2 clone sandboxes / 1 template; the production entry-point direct-drive test and user-reachable E4023 serve as evidence                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | B     |
| I8 Verifiable equivalence | Every slice has zero corpus/IR-snapshot drift or item-by-item classification; budget/rollback have measurements                                                                         | All completed slices have records: **6.9.3d has been connected to production decision points (d is complete, I7)**; **6.9.4 has been established** (2 corpus entries + 1 snapshot + baseline +10/-0, including the 8-line B.4 debt record) + **the F-1/T23 fix round** (+2 corpus entries +1 snapshot, base 402/402 with an empty diff); **6.9.5 has been established** (B.1–B.4 + residual supplementary tests, fast 2279 entries; the debt was recorded with this line); **C3 has been established** (`c1ac5b0c`: M1 open-item chain 8, category-D bytecode `CallStatic=factorial(::4)`, 402/402 with an empty diff, zero IR drift, fast 2303)                                                                                                                                                  | E     |

| E Acceptance           | I8           | Acceptance for every slice + disposition of #537/#540/#541/#542                                                                                                   | Cross-cutting                                                                                               |

> **6.9 closure criterion**: I1–I5 and I7 all hold; I6 closes according to the design-review
> conclusion for 6.9.5 (does not block P7); I8 has evidence for every slice; residual items
> #537/#539/#540/#541/#542/#552 all have a disposition (fixed / explicitly excluded / separate
> case); #542 landed on 2026-11-19. **Do not substitute “all subphases are checked” for the
> invariants holding.**

> **Partial order (not a serial chain)**: A facts → {A.C3, B.d, C.2, D design}; B kernel → {B.d,
> C.2, D implementation}; C.1 is independent and complete; F4 ruling → A.C3; decision-point protocol
> → B.d; design review → D implementation. A.C3 and B.d may run in parallel but require file-surface
> coordination (C3 leans toward typecheck→mono, d toward types/solver→sema), C.2 may run in parallel
> with B.d after 6.9.2-B/6.9.3a (it does not depend on defeq); D’s implementation waits for
> confirmation of the B design package; it depends on A (q=0 erasure) and C.1 (dispatch), and may
> depend on the typed exit in 6.9.3. **P7 gate = the A/B/C exits; D does not block.**

> **A.C3 prerequisite: F4 ruling (A was ruled on 2026-11-16; measurement)**: the old wording,
> “`CheckOnly` evidence does not enter the request,” is obsolete—`ConstEvidence`/`CheckOnly` have
> been deleted; the current mechanism is that open-term `normalize` returns `Unproven` ⇒ no request
> is created, and the representation layer `GenericArg::Const(ConstExpr)` can already hold `k-1`.
> **The ruling changed to two orthogonal decisions + one termination subdecision**: D-a
> representation (A: open terms enter `deferred` and are normalized before enqueueing / B: no
> specialization), D-b evaluation point (mono only; C-1’s second field and C-2’s reconstruction from
> IR both break the single representation/evaluator, so C-3 = A); **F4-T termination** (naive firing
> pushes the recursive chain into `max_depth` and hits E3005; T-a: fold the condition within the
> instance / T-b: bounded firing + fallback to the original at the boundary / T-c: a termination
> layer or a specification change). **Measure first, then rule; before measurement, “A is already
> implementable” must not be treated as a conclusion.** **User (2026-11-16): D-a=A-closed, D-b=C-3,
> F4-T=T-b, bound=8; C3 implementation is a separate task; do not claim the complete form of I4.**

> **B.d prerequisite: decision-point protocol (not yet documented)**: RFC-027 §12.1 specifies that
> “the decision point first follows the unified path; after unified failure, it may use equality,
> but must first roll back the partial bindings produced by the unified attempt.” The implementation
> must answer: is the entry point an outer wrapper around `solver.unify`, or a new `decide` layer;
> may defeq attempt equality only when both sides are ground (with no unbound variables); what are
> the snapshot/restoration boundaries and costs; do unify and defeq share one budget or have
> separate budgets. **Responsibility boundary**: unify continues to own subtyping/Any/Never; defeq
> only decides equality and does not take over subtyping; bidirectional subtyping must not be
> treated as equality (RFC-027 §12.1). **Provide the protocol and production call graph before
> writing code**; 6.9.3c’s wiring contract wasted two rounds by assuming `can_assign` was the
> decision point, and `AssignmentChecker` has zero production instantiations.

> **E acceptance and residuals (I8, cross-cutting)**: every slice must provide ① zero
> corpus/IR-snapshot drift or item-by-item classification; ② `testsuite run fast` + affected
> integration tests; ③ idempotence of the error-code generator (if an emission point/code table is
> changed); ④ classification of behavior changes under D59 and red before green. **Wiring tasks
> additionally require**: production call-graph evidence (which production entry point, how many
> paths, which path is reachable); unit-test reachability must not be used as a substitute for user
> reachability; rollback and budget must have measured numbers. **Residual disposition**: #537
> (category D, silent discard of proof-function formal-parameter refinement; do not fix before a
> ruling, and 6.9.4/6.9.5 must not depend on its fix), #539 (import-surface q=0; a C-phase boundary;
> if it remains open, 6.9.4 covers only module-internal forms), #540 (deep-nesting stack overflow;
> **closed**—3.20.30 was closed on 2026-11-14: the orchestration layer uses a 64MB large-stack
> thread + the parser depth gate MAX_PARSE_DEPTH=1024 + E0019; see the 3.20.30 row in 09), #541
> (TailCall; **closed**—on 2026-11-16, three sites advanced and retreated together:
> `call_form_is_rewritten` / `replace_calls_in_function` / `drop_erased_call_args` cover
> `Call`/`TailCall` through the single-point `rewritten_call_target`; the init section and function
> body share one site-rewriting implementation; red→green + three mutations), #542 (orphaned shared
> layer; **implemented (2026-11-19)**: DCE pass + the two post-mono entry points connected + C2c
> temporary workaround removed; red→green, 402/402, and function-table changes attributed with
> deviations), #552 (imported multi-parameter predicate proof execution; a 6.9.5 neighbor, separate
> case). **Ownership awaiting a ruling**: the four open-constraint tripwires in
> `rfc027_refined_transparency` (`Eq(N, n)`) do not belong to 6.9.4’s constant-equality bridge;
> decide whether to leave them in 6.9.4’s outOfScope or move them to 6.9.1/E4018’s proof-function
> surface.

> **Non-claims / boundaries**: this item covers only records Σ (B: witness field) and does not touch
> Π/`DepFn`; it does not claim the complete form of Π/Σ; 6.9.4 covers only constant equality and
> does not deliver the open-constraint `Eq(N,n)` tripwire; the second value position of `f(y)(y)` is
> not part of this item; witness erasure ≠ runtime-check erasure; if #537 is not fixed, 6.9.4/6.9.5
> cover only forms that do not depend on proof-function formal-parameter refinement; if #539 is not
> fixed, 6.9.4 covers only module-internal forms; #540 is closed (3.20.30); #542 is implemented
> (2026-11-19); the inner-isolation red state in 2.1.4 was cleared on 2026-11-20 (the criterion was
> rewritten by slot identity in the 2.1.4 closure) ⇒ **all prerequisites for promoting `verify` to a
> gate have been removed**.

**Hard acceptance** (**2026-10-27 revision, following 2026-10-26 ruling ②**):
`git diff --stat tests/yaoxiang tests/baselines src/std/` must be empty in **each round of
6.1–6.7**. **Direct-drive test files** under `tests/` (tests that directly exercise the changed
code, such as `tests/formatter_integration.rs`) may be modified when mechanically required by the
change, but each modification must be registered with **file / line count / rationale**; **tests
must not be deleted, and assertions must not be weakened**. **Exception**: 6.8.2 is a
**behavior-fix** batch (new diagnostic code + baseline update), does not fall under this hard
acceptance criterion, and is handled as a separate round with a separate ruling.

> **Revision relationship**: the original wording of 2026-10-26 ruling ② was
> `git diff --stat tests/ src/std/`, treating all of `tests/` as requiring zero differences; the
> revised wording **changes only direct-drive test files from “zero differences” to “may be changed
> but must register file / line count / rationale”**, while the **corpus layer (`tests/yaoxiang`),
> baseline layer (`tests/baselines`), and standard library (`src/std/`) retain the zero-difference
> requirement**. First application = 6.1.2: only `tests/formatter_integration.rs` was changed, by
> **3 lines** (2 instances of `assert_format_eq` changed from `i64` to `Int64` + 1 comment line),
> with the rationale = the expected type-name spelling changed by the code change, so the assertion
> must be synchronized; no tests were deleted and no assertions were weakened. See “P6 6.1 Name
> Normalization Closure.”

### P7 Convert the Intermediate Representation to SSA

Source: [04](04-ssa.md) §Implementation Points batches a–d, criterion C4, **strictly serial within a
batch**.

- [x] **7.0 Pure-move split** (6 level-3 tasks)

> **7.0 Design basis and boundaries**: the target layout comes from the directory tree and migration
> table in [01-routing](01-routing.md)—`middle/ir/ir_gen.rs` → `middle/lower/`,
> `sema/inference/expressions.rs` → `sema/infer/` (call-resolution family `call/`). This batch
> **only performs pure moves**, with the acceptance criterion being “zero semantic diff apart from
> paths” (manual review file by file) + C1 snapshot zero-diff. It does not conflict with RFC-039’s
> rejection of alternative A, “split files only, without changing the paradigm”: what was rejected
> is using a move **instead of** the paradigm work; this batch is the **prerequisite** for that
> work—7c.1’s `lower/synth.rs` depends on the `lower/` directory being in place, and 7d.1’s
> `generate_call_expr_ir` method-level split operates on smaller files after the move. The
> expressions side follows the ruling (split only the call-resolution family; `statements.rs`
> remains in place); the overall `inference/ → infer/` rename and the consolidation of statements
> belong to the full migration and are outside this batch, pending a separate design round after P7.
> `synth.rs` is a new boundary rather than a move and belongs to 7c.1. Survey baseline (2026-11-12):
> `ir_gen.rs` is 9,804 lines with 148 methods, with the body consisting of one
> `impl AstToIrGenerator` block (approximately 9,250 lines); `expressions.rs` is 6,825 lines with 93
> methods, with the body consisting of one `impl ExpressionInferrer` block (approximately 6,280
> lines); the largest methods are `infer_call_expr` at 1,671 lines and `generate_call_expr_ir` at
> 866 lines (the latter is the 7d.1 target).

### P9 Anti-Rebound Gates (Unified Script List — Original Gap G3 Supplied by This Document)

Source: [01](01-routing.md) §Anti-Rebound Mechanism + [08](08-maintenance-mechanism.md)
§Machine-Checkable Rules

**This is the sole authoritative list of all CI scripts** (10 active scripts; the 9.8 row is
retained as a cancellation registration). Scripts are connected to CI at their “introduction phase”;
those marked report-only are converted to hard failures uniformly in P9 (changing “report” to
“fail”). Existing violations (parallel representations, disambiguation aliases, etc.) must wait
until P6/P8 to reach zero; converting them to hard failures early would leave CI permanently red and
cause gates to be bypassed manually. When P9 converts them to hard failures, the gates should be
exactly green. P2’s 2.5, P4’s 4.3.2, and P7’s 7c.1 all reference this table and do not establish
separate lists.
